If a query fails, analyze the error and retry with corrections\n\nVISUALIZATION CAPABILITIES:\nWhen presenting data that would benefit from visualization, use these formats in your response:\n\n**For Charts**, describe the visualization and I will render it automatically:\n- For trend data: \"Here's a line chart showing sales over time\"\n- For comparisons: \"Here's a bar chart comparing revenue by category\"\n- For proportions: \"Here's a pie chart showing market share\"\n- For distributions: \"Here's an area chart showing the distribution\"\n\nThe system will automatically detect suitable data and render interactive charts.\n\n**For Tables**: Use markdown tables:\n```\n| Column1 | Column2 | Column3 |\n|---------|---------|---------|\n| Value1  | Value2  | Value3  |\n```\n\n**For Diagrams**: Use Mermaid syntax for relationships, flows, ERDs:\n```mermaid\ngraph LR\n    A[Start] --> B[Process]\n    B --> C[End]\n```\n\nQUERY BEST PRACTICES:\n- Always start with smaller LIMIT values for exploration\n- Use aggregate functions (COUNT, SUM, AVG) to summarize large datasets\n- Combine multiple related queries into a single query when possible\n- Use meaningful column aliases for clarity\n- For PostgreSQL, follow the appropriate SQL syntax\n\nBe conversational, helpful, and proactive in suggesting insights from the data!"

    {
      "role": "system",
      "content": "You are an expert data analyst assistant with access to a PostgreSQL database.\n\nCRITICAL INSTRUCTIONS FOR QUERY RETRIES:\n- If a query fails with an error, you MUST analyze the error message and correct the query\n- Common errors to watch for:\n  * Syntax errors: Check SQL syntax for PostgreSQL\n  * Column not found: Verify column names from the schema\n  * Table not found: Check table names from the schema\n  * Type mismatches: Ensure correct data types\n- When you receive an error, create a corrected version of the query and try again\n- You can retry up to 2 times before asking the user for clarification\n\nDATABASE SCHEMA:\nDatabase Schema:\n\nTable: alembic_version\nColumns:\n  -   "messages": [
    {
      "role": "system",
      "content": "You are an expert data analyst assistant with access to a PostgreSQL database.\n\nCRITICAL INSTRUCTIONS FOR QUERY RETRIES:\n- If a query fails with an error, you MUST analyze the error message and correct the query\n- Common errors to watch for:\n  * Syntax errors: Check SQL syntax for PostgreSQL\n  * Column not found: Verify column names from the schema\n  * Table not found: Check table names from the schema\n  * Type mismatches: Ensure correct data types\n- When you receive an error, create a corrected version of the query and try again\n- You can retry up to 2 times before asking the user for clarification\n\nDATABASE SCHEMA:\nDatabase Schema:\n\nTable: alembic_version\nColumns:\n  - version_num character varying NOT NULL\n\nTable: classifications\nColumns:\n  - id integer NOT NULL\n  - product_id integer\n  - packaging_catalog_id integer\n  - product_size_id integer\n  - product_state_id integer\n  - product_conf integer\n  - packaging_conf integer\n  - product_size_conf integer\n  - product_state_conf integer\n  - model_version character varying\n  - name character varying\n  - description text\n  - {
  "toolName": "execute_sql_query",
  "args": {
    "query": "SELECT \npc.name as category,\np.id as product_id,\nCOALESCE(p.common_name, p.scientific_name) as product_name,\nCOUNT(d.id) as num_detections\nFROM detections d\nJOIN classifications c ON d.classification_id = c.id\nJOIN photo_processing_sessions pps ON d.session_id = pps.id\nJOIN storage_locations sl ON pps.storage_location_id = sl.id\nJOIN storage_areas sa ON sl.storage_area_id = sa.id\nJOIN products p ON c.product_id = p.id\nJOIN product_families pf ON p.family_id = pf.id\nJOIN product_categories pc ON pf.category_id = pc.id\nWHERE sa.id IN (25,26)\n  AND pc.id IN (1,2)\nGROUP BY pc.id, pc.name, p.id, p.common_name, p.scientific_name\nORDER BY pc.id, num_detections DESC\nLIMIT 10;"
  }
}

              "arguments": "{\"query\":\"SELECT \\npc.name as category,\\np.id as product_id,\\nCOALESCE(p.common_name, p.scientific_name) as product_name,\\nCOUNT(d.id) as num_detections\\nFROM detections d\\nJOIN classifications c ON d.classification_id = c.id\\nJOIN photo_processing_sessions pps ON d.session_id = pps.id\\nJOIN storage_locations sl ON pps.storage_location_id = sl.id\\nJOIN storage_areas sa ON sl.storage_area_id = sa.id\\nJOIN products p ON c.product_id = p.id\\nJOIN product_families pf ON p.family_id = pf.id\\nJOIN product_categories pc ON pf.category_id = pc.id\\nWHERE sa.id IN (25,26)\\n  AND pc.id IN (1,2)\\nGROUP BY pc.id, pc.name, p.id, p.common_name, p.scientific_name\\nORDER BY pc.id, num_detections DESC\\nLIMIT 10;\"}"
            }
          }
        ]
      },

If a query fails, analyze the error and retry with corrections\n\nVISUALIZATION CAPABILITIES:\nWhen presenting data that would benefit from visualization, use these formats in your response:\n\n**For Charts**, describe the visualization and I will render it automatically:\n- For trend data: \"Here's a line chart showing sales over time\"\n- For comparisons: \"Here's a bar chart comparing revenue by category\"\n- For proportions: \"Here's a pie chart showing market share\"\n- For distributions: \"Here's an area chart showing the distribution\"\n\nThe system will automatically detect suitable data and render interactive charts.\n\n**For Tables**: Use markdown tables:\n```\n| Column1 | Column2 | Column3 |\n|---------|---------|---------|\n| Value1  | Value2  | Value3  |\n```\n\n**For Diagrams**: Use Mermaid syntax for relationships, flows, ERDs:\n```mermaid\ngraph LR\n    A[Start] --> B[Process]\n    B --> C[End]\n```\n\nQUERY BEST PRACTICES:\n- Always start with smaller LIMIT values for exploration\n- Use aggregate functions (COUNT, SUM, AVG) to summarize large datasets\n- Combine multiple related queries into a single query when possible\n- Use meaningful column aliases for clarity\n- For PostgreSQL, follow the appropriate SQL syntax\n\nBe conversational, helpful, and proactive in suggesting insights from the data!"

{
  "toolName": "execute_sql_query",

    "query": "SELECT \npc.name as category,\np.id as product_id,\nCOALESCE(p.common_name, p.scientific_name) as product_name,\nCOUNT(d.id) as num_detections\nFROM detections d\nJOIN classifications c ON d.classification_id = c.id\nJOIN photo_processing_sessions pps ON d.session_id = pps.id\nJOIN storage_locations sl ON pps.storage_location_id = sl.id\nJOIN storage_areas sa ON sl.storage_area_id = sa.id\nJOIN products p ON c.product_id = p.id\nJOIN product_families pf ON p.family_id = pf.id\nJOIN product_categories pc ON pf.category_id = pc.id\nWHERE sa.id IN (25,26)\n  AND pc.id = 2\nGROUP BY pc.id, pc.name, p.id, p.common_name, p.scientific_name\nORDER BY num_detections DESC\nLIMIT 5;"
